MSCI 降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待
2026 年 10 月 27 日
一、 市場動態
1. MSCI 擬將印尼降至獨立市場，降評風險明顯升高：MSCI 於 10 月 15 日宣布，啟動
將印尼股市由「新興市場（Emerging Market）」 直接降至「獨立市場（Standalone
Market）」的諮詢程序，意見徵詢將於 2027 年 4 月 30 日截止，最晚於 2027 年 6 月
30 日公布最終決定。MSCI 原先於今年 1 月提出可能降至「邊境市場（Frontier Market
）」，此次則跳過「邊境市場」提出直接將該國股市降至「獨立市場」的方案，顯示對

印尼市場治理的疑慮進一步加深。雖然「邊境市場」仍可能是替代分類方案，但 MSCI
強調，除非有明確證據顯示市場治理及運作出現實質改善，否則預期將依目前提案重
新分類，顯示此次降評風險較過去顯著升高。
2. 股權透明度與交易秩序問題為 MSCI 主要疑慮：MSCI 指出，印尼股市長期存在股權
結構不透明、實際自由流通股比例難以確認、疑似協調交易，以及市場監理執法成效
不足等問題，影響國際投資人對市場公平性與可投資性的信心。雖然印尼主管機關已
提出強化持股超過 1%股東資訊揭露、建立高持股集中度（HSC）監理機制，以及規
劃逐步將最低自由流通股比例提高至 15%等改革措施，但 MSCI 認為相關改革尚未展
現持續且具實質性的改善成果。由於問題涉及市場制度與治理結構，改善所需時間可
能較一般短期市場波動更長。
3. 股市反彈後再度回落，外資撤離壓力仍高：受降評消息衝擊，印尼雅加達綜合指數 10
月 16 日重挫 11.57%，創 1998 年以來最大單日跌幅，19 日再跌 3.64%，盤中最低

觸及 4,907.37 點。雖然 20 日至 23 日連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度下跌 1.22%
，收於 5,373.09 點，各產業類股全面收黑，顯示市場信心仍不穩定。根據 Reuters
報導，截至 10 月 19 日，2026 年外資已累計淨賣超印尼股票約 48.8 億美元。此外，
印尼證交所 26 日宣布，將引進獨立專家及外國投資人代表參與高持股集中度審查，
並自 11 月起加強調查疑似協調交易，必要時凍結可疑帳戶，以回應 MSCI 對市場制
度的疑慮。
二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 股市：潛在指數剔除與資金外流，持續壓抑市場評價
若印尼股市最終降至「獨立市場」，原則上將不再納入 MSCI 新興市場及邊境市場相關
指數，追蹤相關指數的被動式基金將須依規則調整持股，實際處理方式仍以 MSCI 後
續公告為準。若改降至「邊境市場」，則可能獲得部分邊境市場資金承接，衝擊相對較
小。Reuters 引述多家金融機構估計，印尼股市降至獨立市場可能引發約 30 億至 60

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
億美元被動資金流出，其中 UBS 估計約 40 億美元，並指出主動型基金另有約 150 億
美元潛在撤資風險，惟實際規模仍視最終分類結果及基金策略而定。
2. 匯率：外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力
印尼股市的降評風險不僅衝擊股市，也可能透過外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力。印尼
盾 10 月 16 日盤中一度貶至 18,070 兌 1 美元，為 7 月下旬以來最弱水準之一，當日
收盤約 18,000。若後續國際資金持續流出，可能進一步增加股匯市波動，對以外幣計
價、且未完全避險的印尼基金及 ETF 形成額外壓力。雖然印尼央行可透過匯市干預及

流動性管理措施穩定金融市場，但在資金外流壓力尚未緩解前，印尼盾仍可能維持偏
弱格局。
3. 基金與 ETF：指數權重下滑，單一市場曝險風險升高
MSCI 相關指數調整已對印尼股票產生實質影響。Reuters 引用 LSEG 資料顯示，印尼
在 MSCI 新興市場指數的權重已由 2026 年初的 1.16%降至 9 月底的 0.43%，相關成
分股亦由年初 18 檔減至 9 檔，反映部分指數資金調整壓力已提前出現。對印尼單一
國家基金及 ETF 而言，主要風險來自股市下跌、資金流出及印尼盾波動；主動式基金
雖可透過選股降低部分影響，但仍難以完全迴避整體市場風險。不同產品實際受到的
影響，仍應依追蹤指數、主要持股及匯率避險安排個別評估。
三、 市場看法：
1. 制度性問題難以速解，降評風險將持續牽動市場：我們認為，此次 MSCI 降評風險並
非單純反映經濟成長放緩或企業獲利不佳，而是涉及市場透明度、交易公平性與監理
執法等結構性問題。尤其 MSCI 不僅啟動降評諮詢，更持續限制印尼股票的部分指數

調整，包括不提高外資納入因子、不新增相關成分股，以及不進行向上規模分類調整
，顯示市場可投資性疑慮尚未獲得有效解決。儘管政府試圖推動改革，但制度修正仍
須經過執行與成效驗證，才能重新取得國際投資人的信任，因此短期內降評風險仍難
完全消除。
2. 外資撤離與指數權重下降，市場信心修復恐需較長時間：印尼今年以來外資已大幅賣
超，MSCI 新興市場指數中的權重亦明顯下降，顯示國際投資人對當地市場的疑慮已
逐漸反映於實際資金流向。我們認為，即使印尼最終成功避免降評，部分國際機構投
資人仍可能因市場透明度、流動性及治理品質疑慮而維持較低曝險。此外，MSCI 對
部分指數調整的凍結措施尚未解除，短期內新增指數資金流入機會仍受到限制，預期
國際資金回流與市場評價修復均可能需要較長時間。
3. 監理改革力度加大，但尚不足以扭轉保守展望：印尼政府近期持續加強改革，包括提
高資訊透明度、強化交易監控、對違規交易開罰，以及引進外部獨立人士參與監理審
查。10 月 26 日該國政府進一步提出主動調查疑似協調交易及凍結可疑帳戶等措施，

11 月 2 日起亦規劃將高持股集中度股票排除於雅加達綜合指數之外。相關改革有助改

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
善交易秩序及短期市場信心，但部分措施仍待正式實施，且實際成效尚未獲得 MSCI
認可。考量制度性問題改善需要時間，政策利多雖可能帶動短線反彈，但尚不足以改
變對印尼股市中期前景的保守判斷。
4. 股市跌深不代表利空出盡，中期風險報酬吸引力偏低：印尼股市在 10 月 16 日及 19
日大幅修正後，雖曾連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度回落，顯示市場尚未形成穩
定回升趨勢。我們認為，當前股市面臨的不僅是短期賣壓，更包括國際投資人信任下
降及風險溢價提高，即使企業基本面未同步惡化，仍可能因評價下修而承受股價壓力

。因此，短線不排除政策利多或跌深後出現技術性反彈，但在 MSCI 態度、外資流向
與印尼盾走勢明顯改善前，反彈延續性仍有疑慮，印尼股市中期展望維持保守。
四、市場展望：
1. 印尼股市維持保守展望，單一市場風險報酬吸引力不足：考量 MSCI 降評風險尚未解除、
外資撤離壓力持續，以及市場治理改革仍有待驗證，我們認為印尼股市未來數季的風險報
酬吸引力相對有限。即使近期股價明顯回落，仍難認定相關風險已充分反映。相較市場制
度成熟、國際資金流動性較佳的新興市場，印尼股市可能持續面臨較高風險溢價，因此對
單一印尼市場的集中曝險應維持審慎態度。
2. 此事件對新興市場指數與東協指數影響相對有限：根據 MSCI 資料，截至 2026 年 9 月底
，印尼股市在 MSCI Emerging Market 指數與 MSCI AC ASEAN 指數中的權重分別約為
0.43%與 7.55%，因此我們認為印尼潛在降評對相關指數的直接衝擊相對有限。此外，新
興市場各國經濟結構與產業組成有所差異，區域型基金可透過跨國分散降低單一市場風險

。惟後續仍須留意印尼事件對整體新興市場投資信心造成的外溢影響，以及全球貿易與匯
率波動風險。

市場焦點
美國
• 9月ISM服務業PMI降至54.9，雖商業活動與新訂單放緩，但就業回升至50.1、訂單積壓增加，
顯示服務需求仍具韌性；另一方面，價格指數升至74.0，創2022年7月以來新高，供應商交貨
速度亦放緩，反映燃料、原物料及供應鏈成本升高，景氣未明顯轉弱之際，通膨壓力反而重
新升溫；同時全球長債殖利率同步走高，AI基建投資延續企業成長動能，也提高資本需求。
整體而言，美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱，但通膨與長端利率壓力同步升高，使金融條件維持偏緊。
中國
• 彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與AI普及下，中國產業快速發展出現供給過剩，產業獲利
能力面臨挑戰，未來三年中國AI產業將可能處於虧損狀態，不過即使模型價格下降，AI使用量
增加將帶動Token需求大幅成長，其中具有企業客戶、專有資料及生態優勢的AI龍頭，將比單

純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。
黃金
• 歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵，加上長期通膨風險升溫，宏觀不確定性讓實體黃金
市場逆勢吸金。本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易，實體黃金的基本面未變，有望為金價帶來中
期的支撐。建議投資人逢修正分批進場，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金ETF(GLD)，以投資組
合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。

美國9月ISM服務需求略降、成本壓力升高，景氣韌性與通膨風險並存
• 服務業維持擴張，需求略降但就業回穩：9月ISM服務業指數降至54.9(前月55.4)，連續27個月位於擴張區間；商業活動
指數降至56.5、新訂單降至59.8，顯示服務需求較前月放緩，但就業指數回升至50.1、重返擴張區間，訂單積壓升至
56.6，顯示內需仍具韌性，服務業尚未出現明顯轉弱訊號。
• 成本與供應鏈壓力升高，通膨降溫面臨干擾：價格指數升至74.0，創2022年7月以來新高，且為7個月來第6次高於70；
供應商交貨指數升至53.2，顯示交貨速度放緩，燃料、柴油、鋼材、銅及記憶體等成本上升，加上供應受限，使服務業
投入成本壓力擴大，通膨降溫進程面臨干擾。
• 市場觀察：美國服務業為經濟成長的重要支撐，目前經濟呈現「需求仍強、通膨升溫」的拉鋸格局，雖服務業景氣支撐

美國企業獲利，但成本上升可能推升利率波動，投資上宜偏向美國短中天期投資級債，兼顧較高收益率與利率風險控管；
股票則可聚焦具定價能力、獲利與現金流穩健的優質企業。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6 資料來源：ISM Services PMI Report， 2026.10.6

全球利率同步走高、AI投資持續擴張，長端美債壓力仍高
• 全球長債殖利率同步走高，反映利率環境重新定價：美國財政部長貝森特指出，近期美債殖利率攀升與德國、日本等主
要債市同步上行，反映全球資本成本重新定價，而非市場對美國資產失去信心；加上目前尚未出現資金大規模撤離美債
並轉向其他主權債券的現象，因此債市波動較偏向全球利率環境調整，而非金融系統性風險。
• AI投資增加資金需求，支撐較高利率環境：貝森特亦駁斥AI泡沫論，認為微軟、Alphabet及Meta等大型科技企業仍以
實際獲利與現金流支撐持續擴大AI資本支出，不同於過去缺乏基本面的科技泡沫；龐大的資料中心、電力與半導體投資
需求提高整體資金成本，也成為推升長天期利率的重要因素之一。
• 市場觀察：目前市場經濟近況屬於利率高檔、成長尚穩的情境中，因而投資策略建議配置大型科技股、AI基建供應鏈及

受惠利差環境的金融股具相對優勢；債券配置則以中短天期投資級債優先，避免長天期債券受利率上行壓力侵蝕。

中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境
• 中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境：彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與
AI普及下，中國AI產業快速發展，但也逐漸出現供給過剩，使產業獲
利能力面臨挑戰。開源模式雖有助於降低技術門檻、應用普及，但也
使技術優勢難以長期維持，加上中國市場長期習慣低價甚至免費的數
位服務，多數AI業者難以透過訂閱模式獲利，轉而依賴利潤較低的API
服務作為主要收入來源。目前中國已備案大型語言模型(LLM)數量達
988個，包括DeepSeek、Kimi及智譜AI等新創企業快速崛起，進一步
加劇市場競爭。此外API服務逐漸被視為基礎公用服務，即使AI Agent
帶動詞元(Token)需求快速成長，也未必能同步提升獲利，由於開源模
型可由不同雲端業者部署，市場進入門檻降低，業者更難建立長期競

爭優勢。
• 後市展望：考量龐大資本支出與持續價格競爭，未來三年中國AI產業
整體仍可能處於虧損狀態，市場將形成「美國閉源、高毛利」與「中
國開源、低成本」模式，即使模型價格下降，AI使用量增加將帶動
Token需求大幅成長，進而推升資料中心、雲端服務及AI晶片需求，
從投資角度來看，基礎設施供應商(晶片、伺服器、資料中心、雲端)受
惠程度可能高於模型開發商；而具有企業客戶、專有資料及生態優勢
的AI龍頭，將比單純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。

人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台，有望提振中國經濟

• 人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台，有望提振中國經濟和市場信心：日前中國推出購房貸款貼息政策及下調
PSL(抵押補充貸款)利率，代表穩增長政策進一步加碼，有助於提振市場信心與經濟預期，此次政策不僅支持房地產市場，也
將「六張網」建設、科技創新、民營及小微企業，納入重點扶持範圍，透過增加再貸款額度，與提高支持比例，帶動投資、
就業及消費回升。短期A股有望在政策推動下出現築底反彈，其中基建投資、製造業投資、房市銷售及居民消費有機會改善，
同時PSL(抵押補充貸款)降息有助於流動性環境邊際寬鬆，降低市場對經濟增長的疑慮，後市來看企業獲利與信用環境有望逐
步修復，進一步鞏固A股慢牛格局。投資建議上房貸貼息政策有利房地產、建材、家居等產業鏈，金融業受惠資金成本下降與

信貸回升，食品飲料、零售、家電及服務消費則可望受惠於居民消費意願提升。

中國、日本減持美國公債的背後邏輯與差異
• 橋水基金創始人達利歐警告，美國公債正面臨中日兩國減持的風險，數據顯示這項警告已成事實。2026年前七個月，中國
美國公債持倉減少660億美元至6,180億美元；同期日本持倉亦減少820億美元至1.1兆美元，扣除價格變動的估值效應後，
兩國實質持倉均大幅下滑。然而，兩國削減美國公債的背後動機並不相同，這將導致未來的減持軌跡出現明顯分化。
• 中國戰略性長期去美元化：1.降低地緣政治風險與轉向資產多元化，隨著人民幣在國際貿易結算中使用增加，市場流動性提
升，中國對美元儲備依賴度顯著下降。2.美債財政可持續性隱憂，美國政府債務持續攀升，引發對美國長期財政健康疑慮。
3.根據彭博經濟研究模型預測，在漸進式減持情境下，中國的美元儲備(以美債與機構債為主)到2050年可能減少約50%。

• 中國搭配戰術性節奏調整(短期緩和因素)：儘管長期趨勢向下，但目前中美利差顯著(美中30年期公債利差超過350個基點)，
在美債殖利率維持高位期間，過快減持意味著實現債券價格下跌的虧損，且可能加劇人民幣升值壓力。因此，中國短中期內
傾向採取「戰略性減持、戰術性放緩」的靈活步調。
美國公債持倉：中國 vs 日本
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.8，美國財政部
中國預計將減少美元儲備
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.8，中國央行、國際貨幣基金組織、彭博經濟研究。

日本受政策利率與本土資本回流驅動
• 與中國的結構性考量不同，日本的減持主要出自週期性因素與匯率干預需求。
• 日本央行貨幣政策轉鷹：日本央行正加快升息腳步。隨著日債殖利率上揚(30年期日債殖利率升至約4.2%)，美日利差持續
收窄，吸引日本國內機構資金回流本土主權債市場。
• 匯率干預政策：日本為防範日圓過度貶值，日本財務省傾向出售部分海外美債資產以籌集外匯干預資金。若日圓再次面臨壓
力，此類拋售干預可能持續。因此，隨著日本央行逐步走向政策正常化，日本對美債的持倉大概會延續下滑軌跡。
• 小結與投資建議：美國公債作為全球無風險資產的地位正面臨結構性挑戰，中國的「長期去美元化」與日本的「本土利率回
升資金回流」將形成雙重壓力，這意味著美債殖利率短期內難大幅下行，美國財政部發債成本居高不下，進而推高全球資本

的融資成本。投資債券配置建議採取「中短天期＋階梯式配置」策略，長天期美國公債(20年以上)受發債供給過剩及中日兩
國長期減持影響，價格波動較大。建議投資人適度降低長天期美國公債暴險，轉向防禦性較佳的中短天期美國公債或高品質
高評級公司債，以鎖定當前較高殖利率同時控管利率風險。
殖利率差：美元與人民幣 vs 美元與日圓
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.29

黃金市場資金流向分歧，實體投資有望支撐中長期金價表現
• 利率與匯率因素，壓抑金價近期表現：9月以來，美國公債殖利率飆升、美元指數走揚，由於黃金本身不孳息，資金傾向轉
往固定收益市場，疊加美元走強壓抑國際購買意願，金價自8月底高點至今已下修約11%。
• 9月資金流向分歧，實體投資強、期貨市場弱：根據世界黃金協會統計，9月在金價下跌之際，全球實體黃金ETF卻吸引大量
資金流入，流入約100億美元(67噸)。另一方面，COMEX黃金期貨市場投機部位大幅下降，避險基金與其他機構投資人的
多頭部位明顯減少，造成期貨市場賣壓增加，進一步推動金價下跌。
• 實體黃金市場逆勢吸金反應投資人的避險需求：歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵，加上美伊衝突未解及AI算力需求強
勁，長期通膨風險升溫，宏觀的不確定性讓實體黃金市場逆勢吸金。從數據上來看，本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易，實體黃

金的基本面未變，有望為金價帶來中期的支撐。建議投資人逢修正分批進場，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金ETF(GLD)，以
投資組合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，2026.10.7。

1. 本資料內容僅供內部同仁及客戶參考，非作為任何金融商品或服務之購買或銷售之推介，不得視為買賣有價證券或其他金融商品之要約或要約之引誘。
2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣(DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 澳洲央行決議如預期升息一碼至 4.60%為今年第四度升息，基準利率創 2011 年
以來新高，澳洲央行在政策聲明中表示通膨壓力尚未緩解，不排除在 11、12 月
政策會議進一步升息；另一方面，澳洲 8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%略低
於預期，但年增 4.0%仍高於澳洲央行 2~3%的目標區間。
⚫ 美國第二季美 GDP 終值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%，其中實質個人消費支出創下 2025
年 3 月以來最大單月增幅，顯示即使高利率持續，美國家庭消費依然具韌性；
另一方面，美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增月增 0.2%、3.0%，雙雙低於市場預
期的 0.3%、3.3%，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人，失
業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，8 月非農就業人數亦同步下修至 13.3 萬人，通膨下滑
和勞動力市場略有降溫，降低市場對聯準會 10 月升息預期，Fedwatch 顯示 10

月升息機率下降至 20%左右。
⚫ 受美伊衝突引發的能源價格衝擊影響，9 月歐元地區消費者物價調和指數(HICP)
整體上升速度遠超預期，其中，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%，歐元區年增
3.8%，均高於歐洲央行的 2%目標，反映能源價格的衝擊持續影響物價，加深歐
洲央行再次升息的壓力。
⚫ 日本上週公佈貨幣政策會議紀要及央行意見摘要顯示多名委員認為當前政策利
率依然處於中性區間之下，需警惕通膨上行風險，且未來應該以更快速度升息抑
制通膨，然而意見摘要提及日本經濟財政大臣城內實表示希望央行充分審視過
去升息對景氣之影響，市場解讀為政府牽制日本央行未來升息步伐，而財務大臣
片山皋月則重申尊重日本央行的自主性。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.760，低點在 31.946，以 31.830 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方，市場預期聯準
會後續仍有升息的空間，提振美元指數表現，加以外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣走貶
至 31.900 上方；不過適逢月底也是季底，匯價逢高吸引出口商進場賣美金，緩解台
幣貶值的速度；臨近週末，美國 8 月核心 PCE 數據 3%低於預期 3.3%，但市場等待
9 月非農就業數據，交投偏向謹慎觀望，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.830。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略有
減弱，市場對於 10 月升息的預期下降，但仍預期 12 月會再度升息。美元指數雖然
脫離高點，但仍在 102 附近高位震盪，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台股再創收盤新高且外資轉為買超台股，且隨著 AI 需求持續帶動台灣經濟出口表
現強勁，預計出口商後續拋匯需求將持續提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，預估本週台幣
區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪偏升。隨著美中兩國完成新一輪談判，雙方同意延長休兵，從
原定的 11 月延長到明年 1 月，降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險，支撐人民幣的走勢由
6.7170 走升至 6.7030；不過由於美伊戰事持續，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方，
加以美元指數及美債收益率雙雙走高，令人民幣由升轉貶至 6.7200 上方；臨近週末，
美國公布 9 月非農數據降溫，聯準會 10 月升息預期下滑，美元指數回落，境外人民
幣走升至 6.7000 週內高點，終場境外人民幣收在 6.7061，週線升值 166 點，境內人
民幣於十一長假前夕收盤於在 6.7053，週線升值 72 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間震盪。就國際市場來看，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，遠低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略
為減弱。數據公布後，市場對於聯準會 10 月升息的預期下降，美元指數小幅回落，
有助於人民幣的表現；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價格再度升破 6.7400，有助
於人民幣的走勢，不過 10/1~10/7 是中國十一長假，預計人民幣走勢主要將受國際美
元影響；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線仍向下發散，空頭格
局不變，不過匯價反彈至 20 日均線附近，顯示下行動能暫緩。綜合以上，預估本週
人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7300

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪上行。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，USD/JPY 大致
在 156.50 至 157.85 間波動；週間，適逢日本上半年會計年度結算，出口商拋匯使資
金匯回力道強勁，帶動 USD/JPY 下行最低來到 156.35，但逢低有買盤進場，USD/JPY
回到 157 整數關卡之上，後隨美債殖利率創高、美元指數突破 102 關卡，USD/JPY
上行最高來至 158.45；臨近週末，美國公布 9 月非農就業人數低於預期，USD/JPY
一度回落至 157 下方，終場收在 157.83，週線上漲 57 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本 9 月製造業 PMI 由 54.9
回落至 54.1，增長速度放慢至六個月以來的低位，主因為產出和新訂單增長放緩，
經濟復甦力道可能減緩，不利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%，
美國 9 月非農數據 2.9 萬人均低於預期，通膨和勞動力市場略有降溫使市場對聯準
會升息預期下滑，加之部分聯準會官員表示無需急於進一步升息，Fedwatch 顯示 10
月升息機率降至兩成，然而美國第二季 GDP 上修至 2.2%經濟基本面穩健仍將支撐
美元。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價處於區間波動，且各天期均線
趨平，上方 158.50 有年線、季線壓力，下方 156.25 則有月線支撐，預期短線以區間
震盪可能性較高。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪。

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，EUR/USD 震盪下
行至 1.1310，而在紐約聯儲主席威廉姆斯發表不急於再次升息的偏鴿言論後，
EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1340；週間，美國 8 月 PCE 數據低於預期，EUR/USD 一度反彈
至 1.1380，然而在全球債券拋售潮下，資金回流美元帶動美元指數突破 102 關卡，
EUR/USD 跌破 1.1300 關卡最低來至 1.1214；臨近週末，美國 9 月非農就業人數低
於預期，EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1285 後回落，終場收在 1.1253，週線下跌 138 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元地區消費者
物價調和指數(HICP)，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%、歐元區年增 3.8%，顯示能
源價格衝擊依然使通膨壓力居高不下，市場預期歐洲央行可能在 12 月再度升息，將
帶給歐元部分支撐。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%、月增 0.2%低於預
期，而 9 月非農就業人數 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人，失業率則由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，
就業降溫及通膨回落降低市場對聯準會十月升息的預期，然而第二季美國 GDP 終
值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%，經濟基本面持穩仍有利美元維持強勢。三、技術面而言，在
日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價在各天期均線下方並跌破 1.1300 整數關卡，月線向下穿
過季線形成死亡交叉，顯示短線空方動能較強。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈
現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

局，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.95%~2%。

Zhen Ding Technology
Valuation
We set a DCF-based TP of NT$750. We use a DCF-based valuation to reflect ZDT's ability to generate stable cash flows. Key
assumptions of our DCF model include a risk-free rate of 5.2% (10-year government bond rate), a market risk premium of 7%,
and an equity beta of 0.89 (1-year weekly share price), deriving a WACC of 10.7%. Our model assumes high-single-digit long-

term growth after 2028E and a 21.6% EBIT margin from 2028E with a gradual annual increase. Our TP is equivalent to 27x/17x
2027E/28E EPS and 4.5x/3.8x 2027E/28E BVPS.
Risks
Key downside risks that could cause the stock to trade below our target price include: 1) lower-than-expected
iPhone/Mac/iPad end-demand; 2) lower content value for new smartphone models or pricing pressure; 3) ZDT losing market
share from its FPC/HDI peers; 4) a slower demand in the substrate industry; 5) failure to pass the qualifications of major AI

GPU or ASIC customers.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

 400G 磊晶、QD Laser 的先鋒

   公司簡介 
英特磊科技股份有限公司(IET-KY)於 2011 年 4 月設立，為全球磷化銦(InP)、銻化鎵
(GaAs)、銻化鎵(GaSb)磊晶片代工領導廠，提供無線通訊、光纖通訊等產品應用。3Q26
營收組成為 GaAs 36.5%、InP 33.4%、GaSb 和勞務收入 26.5%與其它 3.5%。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
區間操作 424 
【前次投資建議】無 
【大盤指數/股價】43092 / 400

➢ 3Q26 出貨量回升，4Q26 認列設備營收，2026 年營收年增 17.4% 
由於 InP 基板供貨恢復穩定使 Epi-Wafer 出貨量成長，IET-KY 3Q26 營
收 3.2 億元(YoY+26.8%，QoQ+24.6%)。毛利率 38.7%(YoY+7ppt)提
升係因高毛利水準之磊晶片及硬體銷售所帶動。EPS 1.26 元。3Q26 營
收組成為 GaAs 36.5%、InP 33.4%、GaSb 和勞務收入 26.5%與其它
3.5%。各項產品線營運狀況如下：(1)GaAs：射頻與國防應用合計占其
比重約 20%，營收年增 72%。(2)InP：APD/PD 需求極為強勁，但因基
板供應受限，營收年減 2%。考量客製化修改特殊設備營收預計於 12 月
認列，且基板供貨穩定，研究員預期 12 月營收高於 10~11 月，預計
4Q26 營收季增 12.4%至 3.6 億元(YoY+12.7%)，預估 2026 年營收 12.7
億元(YoY+17.4%)，毛利率 40%，EPS 4.85 元。 
➢ 2027 年 400G EAM 與 Driver 開始量產，產能年增 10%至 20% 
展望 2027 年，GaAs 方面，國防訂單與商用訂單同步增加 30%。VCSEL
在新增機台後產能估約增加 40%，預估 GaAs 營收年增 20%至 25%。
第一位客戶已突破 InP 基板限制後，預計 2027 年開始量產 400G EAM
與 Driver。若 2027 年基板供應能維持現有水準，預 期 InP Epi-Wafer 出
貨量將有雙位數成長。在 GaSb 方面，公司著重於生物辨識等創新應用，
並已建置第二條 GaSb 生產線，預計於 2027 年正式量產。配合晶片法
案承諾，公司將於 2027 年購買 2 至 3 台機台，機台總數增至 18 台至
19 台，總產能約增加 10%至 20%，預估 2027 年營收 16.1 億元
(YoY+27.1%)，毛利率 41.6%，EPS 6.40 元。 
➢ 初次建議區間操作，目標價 424 元 
IET-KY 作為全球唯一一家採用 MBE 製程量產磊晶的供應商，客戶包含
多家全球雷射大廠。在產品規格上，公司已踏入 400G EAM 與 Driver、
200G VCSEL、QD Laser 等高規格產品所用之 Epi-Wafer 的研發，且
400G EAM 與 Driver、QD Laser 於 2027 年就會開始出貨，產品進程遠
遠領先同業。然而，在 400G 產品面臨龐大需求時，成本較低的 MOCVD
勢必取代初期採用的 MBE 成為主要技術。參考光通訊產業 Forward PE
評價多落在 20X~50X 區間，以 2028 年 EPS 換算，目前為 49X，評價
偏高。初次建議區間操作，目標價 424 元(2028F PE 52X)

個股報告

公司介紹 
英特磊科技股份有限公司(IET-KY)於 2011 年 4 月設立，為全球磷化銦(InP)、銻化鎵
(GaAs)、銻化鎵(GaSb)磊晶片代工領導廠，是少數採用 MBE 技術從事生產的磊晶供
應商，具有基板到磊晶的垂直整合技術能力，提供無線通訊、衛星通訊、光纖通訊等
商業用途，以及太空與國防上的紅外線感測、夜視、攝影等產品應用。3Q26 營收組
成為 GaAs 36.5%、InP 33.4%、GaSb 和勞務收入 26.5%與其它 3.5%。 
資料來源:國票投顧 
圖 1：IET-KY 營收組成

3Q26 出貨量回升，4Q26 認列設備營收，2026 年營收年增 17.4% 
由於 InP 基板供貨恢復穩定使 Epi-Wafer 出貨量成長，IET-KY 3Q26 營收 3.2 億元
(YoY+26.8%，QoQ+24.6%)。毛利率季增 4.7ppt 至 38.7%(YoY+7ppt)提升係因高
毛利水準之磊晶片及硬體銷售所帶動。EPS 1.26 元。3Q26 營收組成為 GaAs 36.5%、
InP 33.4%、GaSb 和勞務收入 26.5%與其它 3.5%。應用比重為射頻 19.6%、汽車防
撞及車用 13.3%、光纖網路 24.6%、高速傳輸元件 13.7%、紅外線偵測 25.2%、硬體
構件 3.6%。各項產品線營運狀況如下：(1)GaAs：射頻與國防應用合計占其比重約
20%，營收年增 72%。(2)InP：受惠於 5G 基礎建設、高頻光纖網路與雲端運算發展，
APD/PD 需求極為強勁，100G/200G PD 占整體營收 15%至 20%，但因基板供應受
限，營收年減 2%。(3)GaSb：由於國防客戶快速加單，商用訂單持續擴大，3Q26 營
收占比由 15%升至 26.5%，營收年增 18%。考量客製化修改特殊設備營收預計於 12
月認列，且基板供貨穩定，研究員認為 11 月與 10 月營收水準差異不大，12 月營收
高於 11 月，預計 4Q26 營收季增 12.4%至 3.6 億元(YoY+12.7%)，預估 2026 年營
收 12.7 億元(YoY+17.4%)，毛利率 40%，EPS 4.85 元。 
2027 年 m-PIN、400G EAM 與 Driver 開始量產，產能年增 10%至 20% 
展望 2027 年，各產品線發展如下：(1)GaAs：國防訂單與商用訂單同步增加 30%。
m-PIN(變晶型 PIN)可望進入量產階段。VCSEL 在新增機台後產能估約增加 40%。二
次長晶已由淡水廠接單，新機台即將完成驗收，已獲得固定客戶下單並進行認證，2027
年可支應客戶需求，預估 GaAs 營收年增 20%至 25%。(2)InP：第一位客戶已突破 InP

### Page 3

- 3 -

個股報告 
基板限制，預計 2027 年開始量產 400G EAM 與 Driver。在歐洲供應商導入，以及研
磨、晶片表面拋光重複使用等技術逐漸成熟下，若 2027 年基板供應能維持現有水準，
預期 InP Epi-Wafer 出貨量將有雙位數成長。(3)GaSb：公司著重於生物辨識等創新
應用，並已建置第二條 GaSb 生產線，預計於 2027 年正式量產。在公司新增機台後，
GaSb 產能估增加 30%。(4)勞務收入(硬體構件)：客製化新材料機台代營運及「由租
轉買」合約已接近完成。產能方面，在二期廠房擴建工程方面，經過審核與跨政黨行
政團隊過渡，法律與技術問題已全數解決，德州晶片法案補助款亦已陸續領取並支應
擴建工程，預計 4Q26 內完成簽署正式合約，1Q27 完工，可望於 2H27 開始挹注營
收。同時，配合晶片法案承諾，公司將於 2027 年購買 2 至 3 台機台，機台總數增至
18 台至 19 台，總產能約增加 10%至 20%。新增機台將優先配置於 VCSEL 與 GaSb。
假設工廠平均稼動率保持在 70%至 80%，與 2026 年相近，預估 2027 年營收 16.1
億元(YoY+27.1%)，毛利率在 400G EAM 與 Driver、VCSEL 等高毛利產品營收比重
上升下增至 41.6%，EPS 6.40 元。 
IET-KY 可望成為全球第三家 QD Laser Epi-Wafer 供應商 
展望 2028 年，IET-KY 於 2027 年進入試產階段的 QD Laser GaAs Epi-Wafer 可望開
始放量出貨，成為繼 QD Laser Inc.及 Innolume GmbH 之後，全球第三家 QD Laser 
Epi-Wafer 供應商。200G VCSEL 可望從研發階段步入試產或量產階段。在 InP 部分，
第二位 400G EAM 與 Driver 客戶產品將開始量產，與第一位客戶一同貢獻 InP 營收，
加上 1.6T 之 200G PD 出貨比重提升，預期 InP 營收仍會有雙位數成長。生物辨識所
需的 GaSb Epi-Wafer 則因市場持續擴大而出貨年增雙位數，預估 2028 年營收 19.3
億元(YoY+19.8%)，毛利率 42.4%，EPS 8.16 元。 
MBE 是下一代規格的起源，初次建議區間操作，目標價 424 元 
IET-KY 作為全球唯一一家採用 MBE 製程量產磊晶的供應商，客戶包含多家全球雷射
大廠。在產品規格上，已踏入 400G EAM 與 Driver、200G VCSEL、QD Laser 等高
規格產品所用之 Epi-Wafer 的研發，且 400G EAM 與 Driver、QD Laser 於 2027 年
就會開始出貨，產品進程遠遠領先同業。然而，在 400G 等產品面臨龐大需求時，成
本較低的 MOCVD 勢必取代初期採用的 MBE 成為主要技術，因此 IET-KY 也將維持
「營收規模小、毛利高」的路線，以前沿技術出貨高規格產品而穩定成長。參考光通
訊產業 Forward PE 評價多落在 20X~50X 區間，以 2028 年 EPS 換算，目前為 49X，
評價偏高。初次建議區間操作，目標價 424 元(2028F PE 52X)。

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000270] 기아(000270) 배당은 지켜질 것기아(000270) 배당은 지켜질 것배당은 지켜질 것 - 기업.pdf

기아 (000270)

Company Brief 
자동차/모빌리티 
Analyst 김준성 
joonsung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 배현서 
hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 배당은 지켜질 것  
3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY)으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY)을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 150,000원 
현재주가 (10.30) 95,600원 
상승여력 56.9%

Document 304: Brent at $77_bbl_ 3 calls for energy markets

Source PDF: all report pdf/Brent at $77_bbl_ 3 calls for energy markets.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Given the level of uncertainty in energy markets this year and
with little sign of a change going into next year, any call on
energy markets for 2027 is a low-conviction call. But here are
our 3 calls for energy markets for 2027
Warren Patterson
Oil ﬂows are recovering, but uncertainty remains
How to read our three calls for 2027
Source: ING
ING's base call: Brent to average
$77/bbl next year
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.

You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.

### Page 2

Oil markets have been on a roller coaster this year given
events in the Middle East. However, we are going into year-
end with oil ﬂows from the region near pre-war levels despite
the lack of a deal between the US and Iran. While oil ﬂows are
normalising, the way oil is coming out of the region is
anything but normal, which is reﬂected in the surge in tanker
shipping rates, given the very ineﬃcient oil ﬂows.
Our base case assumes occasional ﬂare-ups between the US

and Iran, meaning a full normalisation of Persian Gulf
supplies remains some way oﬀ and the oil market is likely to
retain a degree of risk premium. However, provided there are
no signiﬁcant or prolonged disruptions, we expect the market
surplus to build over the course of 2027, leaving Brent to
average around $77/bbl. A deal between the US and Iran
would add further downside risk to this view.
Our risky call: OPEC will see more member
exits
We believe harmony within OPEC could be tested, with the

possibility of further members leaving the group. This follows the
UAE’s departure earlier this year. Post-Persian Gulf disruptions, any
breakdown or weakening of the group risks adding more supply to
the market, which would potentially leave further downside to our
base case price forecasts for Brent in 2027.
The most likely candidate to leave OPEC is Venezuela, with the US’s
growing inﬂuence over the Venezuelan oil industry following the
capture and arrest of President Maduro at the start of 2026. But we

believe that some Persian Gulf producers may also question their
membership, particularly if disagreements emerge over the
capacity numbers used by the group to set production targets. Iraq
has already made noise around production targets, and following
the exit of the UAE, some members may be looking at them
enviously, with the UAE pumping record volumes of crude since
leaving the bloc.

### Page 3

This publication has been prepared by ING solely for information purposes
irrespective of a particular user's means, ﬁnancial situation or investment
objectives. The information does not constitute investment recommendation,
and nor is it investment, legal or tax advice or an oﬀer or solicitation to
purchase or sell any ﬁnancial instrument.
Read more
Download
Our bold call: TTF to trade sub-€40/MWh
before the end of next year
European gas prices have strengthened signiﬁcantly in 2026. But

what goes up must come down, and we believe prices could fall
sharply next year. There are clearly plenty of assumptions here,
most importantly a normalisation of Persian Gulf LNG ﬂows in
early 2027. If that happens, we believe TTF could trade below
€40/MWh by end-2027.
On the demand side, higher prices are already driving some
industrial demand destruction, while we are deep in the gas-to-coal
switching range. Furthermore, El Niño could bring milder weather to

parts of Europe and East Asia. While far from guaranteed, this could
reduce heating demand in winter. So, we potentially come out of
the 2026/27 winter with the EU gas balance not as tight as many
fear, putting downward pressure on prices.
We also have signiﬁcant US LNG export capacity coming onto the
market, which should help with storage ahead of the 2027/28
winter. If the situation in the Persian Gulf normalises, we also have
new capacity starting up in Qatar.

Document 319: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research – BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

Document 329: Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged Chips & Deflating China

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged Chips & Deflating China.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged Chips & Deflating China
Date:    8 hours ago
Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged Chips & Deflating China

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged
Chips & Deflating China
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 16 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Tonik / Unsplash

I. Cyber Glitches & Chip Controls
"Once is happenstance. Twice is coincidence. Three times is enemy
action." That's according to Ian Fleming’s Goldfinger (1959)

### Page 2

What, then, should we make of the recent string of technology
disruptions? In October, hackers tampered with freezers at roughly 700
French supermarkets, spoiling food and triggering fires. Last Thursday,
Microsoft suffered a 10-hour outage affecting Outlook and Teams. On
Friday, the Federal Aviation Administration (FAA) imposed the longest
nationwide flight ground stop since 9/11. Then, today, customers at major
US banks, including JPMorgan Chase, reported problems accessing their

accounts, while a computer failure temporarily grounded United Airlines
flights.
France blamed Iran's Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps for the
supermarket attacks, although Tehran denies responsibility. The other
disruptions appear to have been technical failures, including software
bugs at Microsoft and JPMorgan Chase and a malfunctioning router at the
FAA. United's outage was resolved within 90 minutes. No evidence links
these incidents, but their timing is curious, to say the least.

Regulatory loopholes might also be considered glitches, and one may
finally be getting fixed. On Monday, the US Commerce Department
announced tighter restrictions to prevent Chinese companies from
accessing American AI chips and computing power through overseas data
centers, particularly in Southeast Asia. The measures follow scrutiny of
Nvidia's ties to Singapore-based Megaspeed, which has been accused of
circumventing US export controls.
The announcement added to the semiconductor stock selloff as investors

worried about lost chip sales and slower overseas data center investment.
The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index (SOX) plunged 5.3% today, while
the iShares Semiconductor ETF (SOXX) fell 5.4%. Nvidia declined 4.1%,
AMD 5.6%, and Micron more than 7%. The Nasdaq Composite dropped
2.5%, remaining below its 200-day moving average (charts).

### Page 3

Investors are particularly worried about Nvidia. How much of its revenue
could tighter export controls put at risk? Will CEO Jensen Huang's
departure unravel Nvidia's circular financing strategy and the broader AI
investment boom? Tomorrow's earnings call, led by interim CEO Colette
Kress, could provide crucial answers.
In any event, money appears to be rotating out of technology and into the
broader market. The S&P 500 equal-weight stock index has outperformed

its market-weight counterpart in recent weeks (chart). The former remains
near its recent record high, while the latter is down 5.1% from its record
high on October 5. The Nasdaq is 11.2% below its October 5 record high.
SOXX is down 35.3% from its June 22 record high. The market is holding
up remarkably well given the shellacking of the semiconductor stocks.

### Page 4

II. China Is Weakening
China's economy continues to lose steam, with domestic demand
particularly weak. Consumers are depressed. Deflation is mounting
because of excess production. Here’s more:
(1) Retail sales & production. Retail sales rose just 0.4% y/y in October.
Inflation-adjusted retail sales fell 0.4%, highlighting persistent weakness in
domestic demand. Both measures are hovering around zero, a sharp
contrast to the double-digit growth rates that prevailed before the

pandemic.
Chinese consumers remain deeply pessimistic, with the consumer
confidence index far below its historical average (chart). They've been hit

### Page 5

by a huge negative wealth effect in the property market. They've also
been losing money in the stock and gold markets this year.
Industrial production rose 4.8% y/y in October despite declining real retail
sales, highlighting the imbalance between China's manufacturing output
and domestic consumption that pushes Chinese producers to dump more
goods onto global markets (chart).
Automobile production remains near record highs at an annualized 27.9
million vehicles, despite weak domestic demand (chart). Total domestic

passenger vehicle retail sales over the trailing 12-month period through
October came to roughly 19.5 million units, down about 20% y/y. The glut
has been exported.

### Page 6

(2) Inflation. China's inflation data further confirm weak domestic demand
and excess production. Headline CPI rose just 0.7% y/y in October, while
prices excluding food and energy declined 0.4% y/y (chart).
The prolonged property downturn is a major drag on Chinese consumers.
New house prices across 70 cities fell 2.4% y/y in October (chart). New
house prices have been falling since 2022.

### Page 7

Financial markets reflect these challenges. Chinese real estate stocks
remain deeply depressed, with the Shenzhen Real Estate Index near
levels last seen during the global financial crisis (chart). The CSI 300 has
been volatile and trendless for years.
(3) Trade. China's manufacturing overcapacity is increasingly spilling into
global markets. Merchandise exports have surged to a record $4.2 trillion
annualized, far outpacing imports of $3.1 trillion (chart).

### Page 8

As a result, China's trade surplus reached a record $1.27 trillion over the
past 12 months (chart).
China's crude oil imports, adjusted for Brent prices, are flattening after
decades of growth (chart). That's largely because more Chinese are
driving electric vehicles. This development was weighing on global oil
prices until the war in the Middle East boosted them this year.

### Page 9

China's exporters are increasingly looking beyond the US, with shipments
to Europe surging to nearly $890 billion over the past year, almost twice
the $463 billion shipped to America (chart).
Exports to Vietnam have also soared, to a record $250 billion, alongside
strong growth in shipments to South Korea and India (chart).

### Page 10

(4) Money & credit. China's credit boom has left its banking system with
enormous outstanding loans of $41.9 trillion, almost three times the $14.2
trillion outstanding at US banks (chart).
Bank loan growth continues to slow, with bank loans and M2 growth rates
in clear downward trends (chart).

### Page 11

Weak bank credit growth reflects the broader economic malaise, even as
Chinese interest rates remain exceptionally low (chart).
The People's Bank of China continues accumulating gold, reflecting its
efforts to diversify official reserves (chart).

### Page 12

clause.

BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE: DOUBLE-DIGIT GROWTH IN BUSINESS CREATIONS,
LOWER INCREASE IN BANKRUPTCIES, AND STABLE NON-PERFORMING LOANS – PARTIAL UPDATE WITH
MONTHLY DATA AS OF SEPTEMBER 2026
ECO CHARTS | BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IS A NEW PUBLICATION AIMED AT PROVIDING AN OVERVIEW OF THE DYNAMICS
OF BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE, AS WELL AS THERISKS ASSOCIATED WITH THE LATTER. WE WILL PUBLISH

REGULAR UPDATES TO THIS PUBLICATION.

### Page 2

Double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans
2ECOCHARTSECONOMIC STUDIES
• Business creations rose by 16% y/y as of Q3 2026, marking an acceleration compared with the growth seen in 2025 (5.3% y/y).
• This increase was driven by sectors associated with new information and communication technologies, where the trend is gaining
momentum.
• Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 1.7% y/y as of Q3 2026, according to the data from the Banque de France (vs. 3.6% y/y in

2025). After a more pronounced rise in the first quarter (8% y/y), this increase has since slowed. This moderation could continue, as,
according to Altares, payment delays have decreased to an average of 12.3 days in the second quarter of 2026 (14.1 days a year
earlier), compared with the European average of 13.1 days.
• The rise in business bankruptcies is most pronounced in the services sector, which has simultaneously seen an increase in business

creations. This trend is clear in the retail sector, with a 26% y/y increase in business creations as of Q3 2026, while business
bankruptcies are up 3% y/y.
• In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been declining since Q2 2026.
• The level of the average ratio of non-performing loans is moderately high and has remained stable for the past year (4.0% of total
loans and advances in Q2 2026). More cyclical than in other sectors, the ratio of non-performing loans in construction reached its

highest level since 2019, at 9.5%.
• The share of underperforming loans, but not yet non-performing (in Stage 2, according to IFRS 9 terminology), which provides an
indication of the future trend in non-performing loans, is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all-time high (16.1%).
• Despite a significant rise since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies — which allows them to be weighted by a proxy for
company size — remains in September, on average, below its Q1 2025 peak (0.54 compared with 0.61, respectively). The economic

weight of bankrupted small businesses decreased but still stands at a high level (2.03).
For more information, please refer to our publication: Chart of the week | French corporates: peaks in creations put peaks in bankruptcies into
perspective. July 22, 2026 – Economic Research – BNP Paribas

Variable Source Frequency Definition
Business bankruptcies Banque de France Monthly
Companies subject to collective proceedings (receivership and judicial liquidation). Monthly data from the
Banque de France aggregated over a quarter to minimise the risk of calendar effects
Business creations Insee Monthly
Survey of business creations (all types, companies, sole traders, micro-entrepreneurs). Monthly data from
INSEE aggregated over a quarter to minimise the risk of calendar effects

Non-performing loans ratio EBA Quarterly
Gross non performing loans and advances at amortised cost / Total gross loans and advances at
amortised cost
Share of underperforming loans (Stage 2 IFRS 9) ECB Quarterly
Loans and advances in Stage 2 / Total loans and advances. Loans and advances at amortised cost and at
fair value through equity
Economic weight of bankruptcies Banque de France Monthly
Total outstanding bank loans to newly bankrupt NFCs over a one-year period, calculated as the figure for

the month in which the firm is declared bankrupt, relative to the average outstanding bank loans to both
healthy and bankrupt NFCs during the same period.
Share of 90+ days past due payments Urssaf Monthly Contributions still to be collected by Urssaf / Amount of contributions due to Urssaf

### Page 2

Macro Outlook 2027: Steady Baseline, Downside Risks

We entered 2026 expecting sturdy global growth and slower inflation. The war in Iran 
derailed this inflation forecast, but the global economy proved more resilient than 
feared at the start of the war. 
Even though global headline inflation is on course to end 2026 at 3.7% on a Q4/Q4 
basis—1.3pp higher than our forecast from last year—we estimate that the global 
economy still grew at a remarkably unremarkable 2.7%, in line with potential and only 
0.1pp below our projections from our 2026 outlook (Exhibit 1). Our interpretation is that 
surging AI capex, easier financial conditions, and (in the US, Germany, and parts of Asia) 
fiscal expansion mostly offset the energy supply shock. As a result of these tailwinds, 
GDP growth only modestly underperformed in the US (-0.3pp), Euro area (-0.3pp), and 
China (-0.2pp) while significantly outperforming in Asian economies such as Taiwan 
(+8.6pp) and Korea (+1.9pp) that benefited from surging AI exports.

Growth Remains Solid but Unremarkable in 2027 
We expect another year of solid but unremarkable GDP growth in 2027, but several of 
the impulses that shaped global growth in 2026 will look different. 
The headwind from the energy supply shock will likely flip next year as energy prices 
normalize. Our commodity strategists’ baseline is that continued Middle East supply 
adaptation will lower Brent prices to $78/bbl by end-2027 with a similar normalization 
in global LNG prices (Exhibit 2). Based on these projections, we forecast that the energy 
price impulse to global growth will swing from a 0.4pp drag on global growth in 2026 to 
a modest boost in 2027 (although we see a continued drag in Europe from delayed 
energy cost pass-through to consumers).

Exhibit 1: Inflation Surged, but Growth Proved Resilient in 2026

### Page 3

A positive growth impulse from energy prices should outweigh the effect of tighter 
financial conditions and a smaller fiscal impulse in 2027. Our GS Financial Conditions 
Index (GS FCI) has tightened by around 50bp in the US and by 30bp in developed 
markets (DMs) over the last three months on the back of lower equity prices and higher 
rates. It now implies a moderate drag on growth over the next few quarters (Exhibit 3). 
We do not expect meaningful fiscal consolidation owing to higher rates in 2027, but the 
large fiscal expansions of the last few years are fading. The main exception is Japan, 
where consumption tax cuts, an increase in defense spending, and the Takaichi 
administration’s investment plans point to a meaningful 0.7pp boost to GDP growth. We 
also expect modestly positive impulses in Germany and the US—where the Democratic 
victory in November’s US election suggests some of the spending cuts from the 2025 
One Big Beautiful Bill Act will be reversed—but a continued fiscal drag in the UK that 
leaves the DM fiscal impulse only slightly positive.

Exhibit 2: Oil Prices Are On Course to Partially Normalize, Which Will Turn a Large Headwind into a Modest Tailwind

### Page 4

The main global growth impulse that we do not expect to change in 2027 is increased AI 
capex. We forecast that nominal global AI investment will rise from $1.1tn in 2026 to 
$1.8tn in 2027, mostly driven by an increase of over $500bn in hardware spending in the 
US (Exhibit 4). We expect the investment surge to be frontloaded, with the 
year-over-year change in investment as a share of GDP peaking in 2027H1 (the level of 
investment as a share of GDP peaks in 2028 under our forecast). Even with the expected 
slowdown, the impact on GDP growth will be sizable: we estimate that AI investment will 
boost GDP growth by 0.4pp globally. And although we again estimate only a small 0.2pp 
boost to measured US GDP growth in 2027 owing to continued undercounting of 
semiconductor investment and chip-related intellectual property exports, we estimate a 
larger 0.6pp boost to “true” GDP growth after correcting for these measurement biases.

Exhibit 3: The Combined Effects of Fiscal Policy and Financial Conditions Are Poised to Turn Roughly Neutral in 2027

### Page 5

The other notable growth impulse from 2026 that will continue in 2027 is a drag on 
rest-of-the-world growth from China. Our forecasts for China remain above consensus 
for 2027 (4.5% GS vs. 4.4% consensus), but this again reflects very robust expectations 
for Chinese exports and a further increase in China’s trade surplus (Exhibit 5). By 
contrast, we expect domestic demand growth to remain weak in 2027 owing to a 1.3pp 
of GDP property sector drag and weak underlying consumer spending, resulting in a 
modest 0.1pp headwind to GDP growth for the rest of the world, with larger growth 
drags in manufacturing-heavy economies such as Germany (-0.3pp) and Central Europe 
(-0.2pp).

### Page 6

Taken together, our 2027 global GDP growth forecast is modestly optimistic at 2.8%, a 
pace that is 0.1pp above our estimate of potential. As has been the case for the last 
several years, we are more optimistic on US growth (2.4% vs. 2.2% consensus) owing to 
resilient investment and more downbeat on the European growth outlook (0.9% vs. 
1.3%) owing to tighter financial conditions and our expectation that higher energy 
prices will start to bite this winter. We also expect continued robust growth in South 
Korea (3.2% vs. 2.7%) and Taiwan (6.3% vs. 5.9%) reflecting another year of strong AI 
exports.

Downside Risks 
We see risks around our baseline global growth forecasts as skewed to the downside for 
three reasons. 
1. A Renewed Energy Shock 
The first and most immediate risk is that an escalation of the war in Iran could lead to 
more significant energy price increases, creating growth headwinds that—unlike in 
2026—FCI and fiscal impulses would not offset in 2027. While our commodity strategists 
see risks around their energy price baselines as two-sided, they put more weight on an 
upside scenario where intensified shipping attacks, damage to oil infrastructure, and a 
more persistent risk premium push oil prices up to $132/bbl (Exhibit 7). If these risks 
materialize, the energy price impulse would remain a moderate drag on global GDP 
growth in 2027.

### Page 7

2. Bond Market Turbulence 
Second, we worry about a bigger fiscal risk premium. In 2026, we saw the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—show 
up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates. Although rates have eased 
somewhat over the last few weeks, the DM yield curve remains 85bp above its level in 
early 2026. If sustained, we estimate that this increase would subtract a cumulative 0.5% 
from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment (Exhibit 
8). Only part of this drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect 
bond yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.

### Page 9

3. A Weaker AI Impulse
The transition to an AI-enabled economy is occurring in two phases. The first phase
involves an investment boom that raises both corporate profits and global GDP growth
but features only limited productivity gains and labor market disruption (because it
takes firms several years to adopt AI and reorganize to take full advantage of the new
technology). The second phase involves lower hardware investment but more visible
business reorganization, as well as more substantial productivity gains and labor market

disruption.
To illustrate this transition, we overlay a simulated AI capex path with our estimates of
the labor market impacts under a 10-year adoption timeline (Exhibit 10). The capex
path assumes that US AI investment follows equity analysts’ projections through 2032,
after which the capital stock converges toward a long-run equilibrium level.1  Under our
baseline calibration, the AI capital stock eventually converges to a long-run level of
around 9% of GDP, and 9% of total US workers are displaced. (We continue to expect

most of these displaced workers to find new jobs, including in new occupations that are
created by AI).
Under these assumptions, the transition from the first phase to the second phase
doesn’t occur until 2028-2029, when we see a further pickup in the underlying US
productivity trend to the 2½-3% range and a gradual increase in the US unemployment
rate to 4½%. A backloaded transition is consistent with the projections by equity
analysts that AI investment will keep growing through 2028, albeit at a slower pace than

in 2026-2027. It is also consistent with the renewed pickup in US employment growth
since the spring, which has temporarily laid to rest concerns around imminent AI-related
job market disruption.
1 We characterize the long-run equilibrium level following Hall and Jorgenson (1967) as a function of the
depreciation rate, technological cost disinflation, nominal interest rates, and the AI capital factor share of total
output.
12 November 2026   9
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 10

But it is hard to be sure about the timing of this shift and we see some signs that it could 
occur earlier.  Hyperscaler equity price reactions to earnings call signals for higher capex 
switched from positive in 2024-2025 to slightly negative in 2026 as the market has 
become concerned about overinvestment (Exhibit 11). Furthermore, upward revisions to 
hyperscaler capex slowed in the Q3 earnings season, which suggests that the AI 
investment cycle peak is shifting more clearly into view.

### Page 11

Beyond the fundamental demand for compute, we see three other near-term downside 
risks for AI investment: 
Capacity constraints – Semiconductor and memory producers are capacity n
constrained with order backlogs through 2028. While capacity investments currently 
underway should eventually alleviate these constraints, a surge in nominal 
investment in 2027 may increasingly raise prices (thereby pushing central banks 
toward more hawkish policy) without boosting real output. 
Credit markets – Global AI-related debt issuance has totaled $687bn so far in 2026 n
while AI-related equity issuance has added another $115bn. While our market 
strategists see adequate capital supply to meet even higher capital demand next 
year, upward pressure on borrowing costs could lead to more meaningful crowd-out 
of other investment in 2027 (vs. only modest crowd-out in 2026). 
Regulation – Concerns regarding regulatory headwinds to investment surged after n
the US midterm elections in early November raised the possibility of data center 
permitting moratoria in several states (particularly in Texas). We do not anticipate 
that regulation will derail 2027 investment, both because moratoria will likely be 
short-lived and because permits for 2027 projects have mostly already been issued. 
But a more extended delay or stringent regulatory shift would raise downside risks to 
AI investment in 2028-2029, which could weigh on growth in 2027 via tighter 
financial conditions. 
Turning to the labor market, we have argued that AI adoption is likely to displace jobs 
and cause a temporary increase in the unemployment rate of around ½ a percentage 
point over the next several years.2  Our baseline expectation is that this increase occurs 
in a backloaded fashion, with only a small effect in 2027 and more notable effects in 
2028-2029.  However, we are starting to see signs that the AI impact is spreading 
2  See “Revisiting the Extent and Timing of AI Labor Displacement,” Global Economics Analyst, October 22, 
2026.

beyond technology to the broader economy. Across nonmanufacturing industries, the 
share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment (correlations were insignificant at the 
start of the year, Exhibit 12).

Moreover, AI-driven job displacement would likely accelerate in a downside growth 
scenario. Exhibit 13 shows that the trend decline in the employment share of routine 
occupations over the past five decades has been highly concentrated in recessions.  
Given that AI has sharply increased the number of occupations exposed to automation, 
we would expect corporate leaders to respond to a slowdown by reducing headcount 
targets more aggressively, putting more upward pressure on the unemployment rate.

### Page 13

To be clear, we still expect AI-related pressure on the labor market to remain temporary.  
Employment in existing industries such as healthcare is likely to keep growing rapidly, 
and history shows that technological revolutions ultimately result in the creation of new 
industries and occupations that absorb workers displaced elsewhere.  Historically, this 
dynamism has kept the unemployment rate from increasing permanently despite all the 
labor-saving technological innovations of the past 250 years.  But over the next few 
years, job market disruption is likely to grow.  
Inflation: Progress Not Perfection 
Inflation progress was slower than expected in 2026 owing to price increases associated 
with the war in Iran.  But under our baseline commodity forecasts for a steady decline in 
energy prices next year, inflation should slow in 2027. 
The case for disinflation is particularly strong in the US because the price boosts from 
tariffs (which are still adding 40bp to year-over-year core PCE inflation through 
October) should fully drop out of the year-over-year inflation statistics next spring 
(Exhibit 14). Combined with a much smaller inflation boost from the war in Iran, we 
expect core PCE to slow from 3.2% at end-2026 to 2.4% at end-2027.

Exhibit 13: Prior Automation Cycles Suggest That Aggregate Automation Speeds Up in

Despite our expectation for progress, we see several reasons why inflation may remain 
somewhat firm in early 2027, thereby delaying a return to target-consistent levels in 
most economies. 
First, while most of the price boost from higher energy prices has been realized, some 
pass-through will spill into next year (Exhibit 15).  This is true both in the US and 
especially in Europe, where indirect effects have so far been very limited.  For example, 
European airfares have not repriced higher in response to fuel cost increases so far—
reflecting fuel hedges, greater competition, and pre-war plans for supply 
expansion—but should pick up notably next year.

### Page 15

Second, global memory price forecasts were again revised higher over the last earnings 
season on the back of robust demand (Exhibit 16).  This pattern both suggests more 
near-term upside to core inflation from AI spillovers—although we still anticipate price 
increases should slow as production capacity increases—and points to upside risks if 
recent pricing trends continue.  This is especially true in the US, where the impact from 
higher memory prices is exaggerated because the weight of the software and 
accessories category is overstated.

Third, upstream cost pressures have increased and broadened (Exhibit 17). These 
increases are accounted for in our baseline forecasts, but historical patterns suggest that 
the recent rise in upstream cost pressures will keep inflation surprise risks elevated 
through the spring. We are particularly concerned about a reacceleration in the US, 
where the “January effect” has driven a seasonal inflation pickup almost every year since 
the pandemic (our forecast incorporates a 0.1pp boost to January core PCE).

We still see most of these price pressures as backward-looking and temporary, however, 
and remain less worried about a sustained inflation overshoot.  We take particular 
comfort in the downward trend in wage growth in most major economies that lowers the 
risk of a pickup in core services inflation (Exhibit 18).  Furthermore, leading indicators 
continue to point toward an incremental softening in US shelter inflation, and we have 
yet to see signs of second-round effects in Europe.

Taken together, we forecast a steady decline in year-over-year core inflation in the US 
and a more delayed decline in other major economies once the energy price shock is 
fully digested (Exhibit 19).  And although core inflation remains somewhat above target 
at end-2027 on a year-over-year basis, we expect that the inflation constraint will

The largest risk to our near-term inflation view is that any renewed escalation in Iran 
could prolong headline (and to some degree core) inflation pressures.  Applying our 
standard rules of thumb to our commodity team’s adverse scenario implies 0.9pp upside 
to headline inflation and 0.2pp upside to core inflation relative to our baseline forecasts 
for 2027. 
Still Expecting Fewer Hikes Than Priced 
We forecast incremental policy tightening through early 2027 in most major economies 
(and see a low bar for modest incremental tightening in others), but our economic 
forecasts support a dovish view on rates in 2027: central banks could either deliver 
gradual normalization cuts later in the year as inflation comes down or pivot to cuts 
sooner if the downside growth risks discussed above materialize. 
In the US, we expect the Fed to deliver rate hikes in December and March in response to 
lingering inflation pressures (Exhibit 20).  But softer inflation data should allow the Fed 
to cut once in December 2027, followed by three more cuts in 2028 to a terminal rate of 
3.25-3.5%.  Furthermore, we see more dovish rate risks in the US than in most other 
economies, both because US inflation drivers look more clearly temporary and because 
the US is most exposed to a downturn in the AI cycle.

### Page 18

Our views on other major DMs also skew dovish (Exhibit 21). We expect the ECB to 
deliver its final hike in December, before starting to normalize back toward a 2.25% 
terminal rate in 2027Q4. And although higher-than-expected inflation could prompt 
another hike in Q1, our softer European growth outlook limits policy rate upside. In the 
UK, we expect a final BoE hike to 4.25% in February before a hold for the remainder of 
2027. We likewise expect normalization cuts from the BoE to a 3.25% terminal rate, but 
not until 2028. Our ECB and BoE forecasts are dovish relative to market pricing on both a 
baseline and a risk-adjusted basis. 
We are also dovish on Canada and Australia. In Canada, we expect two hikes to 3.0% in 
the first half of next year based on recent BoC communication suggesting it plans to 
return to the center of its neutral range (which we believe will be revised up to 2.5-3.5% 
in April).  But with trade uncertainty looming large, it is more likely that the BoC 
underdelivers than hikes above the midpoint of its neutral range (as the market is 
pricing).  In Australia, our RBA forecast for a hold at 4.85% through October is broadly in 
line with market pricing over 2027H1, but more dovish in 2027H2 and especially by 
end-2028 (by which time we expect a cumulative 125bp in cuts to have been delivered).  
The main outlier to our forecast of only modest further policy tightening is Japan. We 
expect the BoJ to deliver another 25bp hike at the December MPM, reflecting recent 
BoJ communication highlighting both upside inflation risks and the impact of higher 
global interest rates on the Yen. But with core CPI (ex fresh food but including energy) 
likely to rise above 3.5% through next summer, we now expect that the BoJ will follow 
through with additional hikes in March and June for a 2.0% terminal rate.

Our views also skew dovish in EMs (Exhibit 22). This is particularly true in CEEMEA and 
LatAm, where we expect most central banks to cut through 2027.  In Asia, we expect 
rates to stay flat or rise slightly (though our forecasts are still below market pricing 
except in Taiwan). Energy prices are the main upside risk to EM rates, but we think 
markets overestimate the inflation risk in most EMs for three reasons. First, inflation was 
already low before the energy shock. Second, EM economies are cyclically weaker than 
DMs. Third, EM currencies have held up well, supported by healthy external balances. 
Some EMs may tighten further over the next few quarters, but we expect most to hold or 
cut after that.

### Page 20

More Challenges for Markets than the Macro
As with the economic outlook, the market outlook continues to be dominated by the
path of the AI cycle and energy risks related to the Iran war.
Our baseline forecast is quite friendly. The mix of steady growth, falling core inflation,
gradually moderating energy prices and an earlier peak in G10 hiking cycles than market
pricing does not signal obvious threats to asset markets. The clearest difference with

market pricing is our more benign view of the policy rate path for many economies,
which all else equal should prove a boost to both equities and bonds. Our central asset
forecasts look for positive returns across the major asset classes. After the recent
sell-off, our forecasts imply robust equity returns between now and the end of 2027, but
we expect the pace of gains to slow as we move through next year.
Despite this baseline, we think the market outlook will continue to prove challenging, as

it has been already for several months. In part, this is because of the risks around our
baseline that we have already described. In part, it is because we think markets—
especially around the AI theme—have been priced in ways that leave them more
vulnerable to signs that important tailwinds may be starting to fade. In the process, the
volatility and rotations that have mostly remained under the surface of major equity and
credit markets this year could spill over more into shifts at the index level.

Managing the Peak in the AI Investment Cycle
The biggest uncertainties—as with the economic outlook—lie around the AI cycle. With
AI investment growth expected to remain robust, the peak in the AI investment impulse
should be a manageable event for the economy. But it is already proving to be a bigger
test for markets for three reasons. First, the AI impulse has been a much larger
contributor to growth in earnings, market value and capital spending than to growth in

GDP. Second, AI-related equities have taken credit for significant macro productivity
gains, especially companies benefiting directly from the infrastructure boom, that have
made them more vulnerable to any signs that we are on a less optimistic path. Third, the
point at which growth rates peak (a “second derivative” turning point) often matters,
even if underlying growth rates (the “first derivative”) remain healthy. Moving through
that peak allows markets to place limits on the upside of an uncertain distribution and as

growth decelerates, the period of positive surprises to expectations often ends too, as
we may be starting to see for the AI capex cycle.
12 November 2026   20
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 21

Markets have already been reflecting these concerns. Since June, we have seen a 
significant derating of companies levered to the AI infrastructure theme, which are most 
sensitive to shifts in the investment outlook and had been the biggest market 
beneficiaries in the first half of 2026. The good news is that robust profits, and a rising 
share of income going to listed companies, have helped keep both the imbalances and 
the deep valuation excesses that we saw in the late 1990s at bay. The key question in this 
cycle is whether the market is correctly assessing how much of the recent acceleration in 
profits, which has taken S&P 500 earnings to a record high relative to GDP, will prove 
persistent especially as the investment cycle itself fades. Without the capital inflows 
from the EM crises in 1997 and 1998—or the rate cuts that goods price deflation 
allowed—increased financing needs have also been putting clearer upward pressure on 
the cost of capital.

Exhibit 23: AI Impulse Has Been a Much Larger Contributor to Growth in US Earnings and 
Market Value than to Growth in US GDP

### Page 22

A Flatter Trend, but Risks of a Bumpier Transition 
Two things give us comfort in terms of how bumpy this transition will prove to be. The 
first is that although the investment impulse from AI is poised to peak, growth in both AI 
investment and earnings should remain firmly positive in 2027, especially earlier in the 
year. We expect S&P 500 earnings growth to decelerate from 36% in 2026 to a 
still-robust 11% in 2027. This may help to limit the de-rating of even the most directly 
affected areas and should reduce the impact on broader growth-sensitive assets. This is 
particularly true since recent anxiety about AI-related returns has pushed the valuations 
of many technology companies lower. The second is that the baton may be passed from 
the direct beneficiaries of the AI investment boom to other areas of the market. This 
includes other areas of the AI complex, such as the hyperscalers, if monetization paths 
become more visible; companies which may begin to show benefits from AI productivity 
enhancements; or non-AI companies which see some relief as the crowding out of 
capital available to them eases (and are now outperforming the broad AI complex in 
2026). 
The risk is that what we expect to be an eventual flattening in the upward trend in asset 
markets—and a rotation away from some of the largest recent winners—becomes a 
bigger aggregate shock. This could happen because the market begins to look further 
forward to the peak in the investment share itself (in 2028 on our forecasts); because 
the passing of the baton to other sectors does not take place as quickly or as smoothly 
as needed; because a fresh energy spike puts non-AI areas back under pressure; or 
because feedback loops from financial and credit conditions accelerate more quickly 
than in our baseline. We think the risk to markets is higher than the risk to the economic 
picture, because the boom itself has had its largest impact there. But tighter financial 
conditions—both in equities and rates—are the most obvious channel through which the 
economic impact could also increase.

### Page 23

A Gradual Shift from Rate Risk to Growth Risk
Our inflation and policy views remain more distinctive than our growth views. With our
central bank views generally more dovish than the markets, particularly as we move
deeper into 2027, our baseline forecast implies scope for front-end pricing to fall
further. The mix of high fiscal deficits and the ongoing AI capex boom limit the scope for
longer-dated yields to fall in our baseline. But if policy rates peak at the lower levels we

expect, forward rates should also move lower (we see the US 10-year yield at 4.6% at
the end of 2027). Faster energy relief would accentuate that process.
This kind of policy relief would be helpful for both equities and bonds. But shifts in the
nature of the risks facing asset markets may be starting to alter the balance of risks
between equities and bonds, and correlations across asset markets, as we move into
2027. Through much of 2026, global bonds failed to protect equity portfolios. The

inflationary impulse from energy disruptions and the surprising resilience of growth and
labor markets in the face of those shocks prompted a sharp shift in policy rate
expectations. The start of a broad-based G10 hiking cycle has pushed real rates sharply
higher in the last 9 months. Under the surface, the mix of rising corporate sector
financing needs with still-high government deficits helped to underpin the upward
pressure on global real yields.

In the near term, the key risks to our baseline economic forecasts still push in that
direction. The most obvious is that any escalation in the Iran war could push energy
prices sharply higher again in the coming months. Particularly after the fall in the risk
premium in oil markets coming into November, a fresh energy shock would hurt equities,
support the US dollar and put renewed upward pressure on rates markets. Recent
firmness in core inflation, and Fed commentary, are also likely to keep worries about a

more front-loaded hiking cycle alive. The impulse from AI investment is also arguably
moving through its strongest point, which may limit any decline in yields.
But the further we move into 2027, the more likely it is that the key risks are positive for
bonds, as the threats from central bank hikes, energy price spikes and the AI investment
tailwind recede and other risks become more prominent. A sharper reversal in the AI
boom or earlier AI-related labor market disruption are the most obvious versions of that

story. But the kinds of financial stresses from a high-rate environment that we saw
emerge in September and October—most obviously in France heading towards the April
election but potentially also elsewhere—would push in a similar direction, as would
“black swan” risks such as a major cybersecurity event.
12 November 2026   23
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 24

Diversification and Protection as the Market Cycle Matures 
With a steady economic backdrop, our baseline forecasts for 2027 are for positive 
returns in equities and bonds, and close to zero returns in commodities. But with a 
maturing market cycle and significant uncertainty around core drivers of the outlook, we 
continue to emphasize the value of diversification and hedges to protect against key 
risks.

We expect ongoing earnings growth alongside the recent valuation compression to 
support further gains in major equity markets in 2027. As the pace of earnings growth 
slows moving into 2027, the pace of US equity gains is likely to drop too, moving well 
below the more than 20% annualized rate of the last three years. With earnings growth

### Page 25

likely to be slower still in 2028, the risk is that the market pulls forward its worries about
a deeper turn in the cycle. Accounting for risks around the baseline view, we recommend
an overweight allocation in the US and Japan, neutral Asia ex Japan/EM, and
underweight in Europe.
We expect further widening in credit spreads in 2027 as the increase in corporate supply
continues. Although widening is modest in our base case, risks are clearly skewed in that

direction. It is hard to see scenarios where US credit spreads tighten sharply (there is
more scope for tightening in Europe where the risk premium has risen more), and easier
to see how spreads could move higher, particularly if economic conditions deteriorate
more than we expect or if investors become more uncomfortable about the profitability
of the AI investment boom. We expect US HY to underperform, with some risk that
problems in the tail of weaker borrowers create more turbulent conditions for broader

financing. Without the large capital inflows and rate cuts prompted by the EM crises in
1997-98, the sustained divergence between credit and equity markets from 1998 to
2000 may more challenging now.
Government bonds offer better returns than in recent years, reflecting higher yields and
improving relative valuations. Real bond yields are at high levels compared with the last
30 years, both absolutely and relative to equities.  Our baseline is for bonds to deliver a

little more than the decent running yield that they currently offer, though the scope for
capital gains is likely to be higher later in 2027. There are risks to that view from fresh
energy shocks, or stickier core inflation than we expect. But if clearer downside risks to
growth emerge, yields could fall more clearly.
After tight ranges in 2026, our baseline is for modest USD weakness as lower inflation
and energy put the USD on the back foot, alongside further appreciation in CNY

underpinned by the surging trade surplus. But if the market challenges US tech
exceptionalism more quickly, we could see a sharper shift away from the USD. And the
same set of growth risks that are increasing the hedging value of bonds would also likely
prompt a sharper appreciation in the Japanese Yen.
Commodities continue to offer value as a portfolio hedge. Despite the ongoing war with
Iran, oil market supply has largely been restored, but risks of escalation and further

disruptions have led to a large but variable risk premium above the oil price justified by
the fundamental supply-demand balance. Our baseline is that this risk premium will
slowly erode, leaving energy and core commodity returns more muted. But there is still
heightened risk of scenarios where energy prices could move much higher, hurting
bonds and equities. While there is also a downside tail to energy prices, if there is faster
progress towards a resolution of the war with Iran, in that scenario equities and bonds

would likely be performing well.
12 November 2026   25
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 26

Alongside diversification, direct hedges are worth considering. Positioning for lower 
rates in the US and Japan, or for jointly lower rates and equities are potential ways to 
protect against deeper downside growth tails. In FX, USD/JPY downside may play a 
similar role, given that it generally performs well when rates and equities are both falling. 
For energy upside, hedges in European gas futures and TTF calls look like asymmetric 
ways to protect against risk. So far, the sharpest volatility has been under the surface of 
the major equity indices. Equity index volatility has risen a little but is unlikely to fall 
much from here and we think has scope to rise further if macro stresses broaden. 
Deeper protection in equities themselves—either through out-of-the-money puts or 
replacing equity holdings with longer-dated call options—also seems more valuable 
than usual as the cycle matures.
